市場焦點
美國經濟
• 第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。美國9月個人消
費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，年增3.6%；核心PCE指數月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對
於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，
持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，
將提高川普後續推動政策的難度。觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年
通常是股市表現最佳的年度，其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對

政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
中國
• 9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快，中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI
硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企業則是能
源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲
弱與價格競爭問題未解，投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。

美國第三季GDP成長超預期、通膨壓力續增
• 美國第三季經濟成長率(GDP)創一年來最大增幅：美國經濟分析局(BEA)公布，第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場
估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。企業AI投資火熱、消費者開支保持韌性，抵銷了油價上漲衝擊。
• 9月數據顯示通膨壓力續增，但符合預期：美國9月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，寫3月以來最大月增幅；年增幅
3.6%。剔除食物與能源的9月核心PCE指數，月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 市場反應：由於新一輪通膨數據符合預期，市場未改變聯準會12月利率決議升息一碼的預測。然而，有跡象顯示實質個人
消費支出開始轉弱，儲蓄率也有所下降，市場開始押注通膨可能已經見頂。從聯邦利率期貨市場數據來看，市場對於明年升

息空間的預期已較上個月收斂(九月底預測升息2~3碼→目前預測1~2碼)，
• 觀點：美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，
可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.30。

聯準會貨幣決議與聲明稿差異
資料來源：Federal Reserve（10/28、9/16 聲明）；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。以上為聲明重點整理。
2 / 5
10月決議｜按兵不動
3.75% – 4.00%
10票贊成、2票反對
經濟措辭轉強 「穩健擴張」改為「強勁擴張」；國內支出具韌性， 生產力與資本投資表現強。
通膨仍未解除 聲明維持「通膨居高」的判斷，未再宣稱本次升息有 助加快回到2%目標。
政策訊號更具彈性 改稱需要更多資訊判斷適當政策立場，未承諾下一步 升息或降息。
關鍵變化
理事庫克(Lisa D. Cook) 和克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth M. Hammack)主張本次升息1碼，
形成2張鷹派反對票；9月決議則為一致通過。利率不變不等於轉向寬鬆，委員會內部對通膨風險仍

有不同判斷。
市場解讀：本次是「暫停觀察」；12月是否再升息，仍取決於通膨、需求與能源價格。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
資料來源：Federal Reserve 10/28會後記者會；Reuters、Investing.com，2026/10/28。
3 / 5
01｜市場利率反映緊縮壓力
華許指出，9月與10月兩次會議之
間，美國公債名目與實質殖利率明
顯走高；市場利率可先行反映成長
、通膨與風險變化，不必等政策利
率調整。
政策含意
長天期殖利率仍受通膨預期、期限
溢酬及公債供需影響；政策利率不
變，不代表債市壓力解除。
02｜AI投資與生產力
華許認為美國經濟尚未過熱，AI投
資推升需求，但生產力也有改善；
聯準會將持續評估新科技對產出、
就業與物價的中長期影響。
政策含意
AI支出可支撐企業成長，也伴隨資本
支出回收與融資風險；應檢視現金流
、投資報酬及生產力是否實際提升。

03｜政策制度與工具再檢視
聯準會規劃2027年安排1次、2028
年安排2次專注貨幣政策策略的會
議，不進行利率表決或發布政策聲
明；另將檢視資產負債表政策。
政策含意
制度調整不等於政策轉向寬鬆；仍須
觀察準備金、資產負債表操作及經濟
數據，評估金融條件如何變化。
解讀：華許除談利率外，也聚焦市場利率、AI生產力及政策制度調整；投資判斷仍應回到數據、企業獲利與金融條件。

聯準會利率決議摘要暨投資策略建議
資料來源：Federal Reserve；Reuters、InvestingLive；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。市場價格可能隨後續交易更新。
4 / 5
政策決議 利率維持 3.75% – 4.00% 經濟強、通膨高；政策下一步保留彈性
美股
短線受利率預期與科技股評價牽動
；AI投資支撐獲利，但仍需檢視現
金流與資本支出回報。
美債
若升息預期升溫，短天期殖利率較
敏感；長天期亦受通膨預期、發債
供給與期限溢酬影響。
美元／黃金
美元可能隨利差預期波動；黃金同
時受實質利率、美元及地緣政治避
險需求影響。
投資策略建議｜情境式配置
股票 保留核心持股，避免追價；優先關注獲利能見度、現金流與定價能力較佳企業。
債券 以分散到期日與信用風險為主；可分批配置高品質債券，避免單押長天期利率下行。

多元資產 保留流動性與再平衡空間；若通膨再升、殖利率上行，分批進場並控管整體波動。
後續觀察：美國通膨與就業數據、能源價格、企業財報，以及市場對12月政策的重新定價。

美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政
資料來源： 各媒體，2002~2022年
歷屆期中選舉確認結果時間
選舉日期 眾議院控制 權確認時間 參議院控制權 確認時間 時長
2006年11月7日 選舉日當晚 11月9日 2天
2010年11月2日 選舉日當晚 11月3日 1天
2014年11月4日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2018年11月6日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2022年11月8日 11月16日 11月12日 8天
• 美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政：美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，為川普第二任期上任以來首場全國性選
舉，除國會議員改選外，亦包括州長及其他公職人員選舉。若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，將提
高川普後續推動政策的難度。不過，川普仍可透過發布行政命令等方式，在一定程度上繞過國會的立法制衡，持續推動其政

策議程。
• 本次選舉結果可能耗費一週才能明朗：回顧2006至2022年近五屆美國期中選舉，其中四屆的參眾兩院控制權於投票當晚或
隔日即大致明朗，僅2022年因關鍵席次競爭激烈、郵寄選票計票耗時較長，直至投票後第8天才確認兩院席次歸屬。考量本
次參議院選情膠著，加上川普執政以來美國政治極化持續加劇，不排除本次選舉結果需耗費一週才能明朗，期間市場也可能
面臨額外的不確定性。

期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度
• 期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度：觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳
的年度。根據1940至2025年統計，標普500指數第三年平均報酬率達16.5%，高於第一年的8.3%、第二年的4.2%及第四年
的6.9%；中位數報酬率更達18.9%。其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，
且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
• 政黨與國會組成影響有限，基本面仍是關鍵： 進一步觀察政黨差異，1940年以來，第三年由共和黨總統執政時，標普500指
數平均報酬率約18.2%，高於民主黨總統執政時的14.9%，但兩者均呈現正報酬，顯示總統所屬政黨並非決定股市表現的唯

一因素。國會組成亦可能影響政策推動與市場預期，例如共和黨總統搭配民主黨國會時，第三年平均報酬率約15.6%；若國
會呈現混合執政，平均報酬率則達23.1%。

能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升
• 能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升：9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快。但前三季度累計增速
從1到8月的15.7%小幅放緩至15.5%，利潤改善主要反映了能源和AI硬體價格的上漲，其中AI硬體價格仍是高技術製造業利潤
的重要支撐。前三季度，高技術製造業利潤年成長52.0%，與1到8月54.7%的增速大體相當。拉動整體利潤成長約10個百分點，
貢獻率約三分之二。值得注意的是，利潤成長仍主要集中於少數相關行業，其他企業部門整體依然處境艱難，內需疲弱疊加價
格競爭激烈令利潤受到擠壓。
• 投資建議：中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企

業則是能源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲弱與價格競爭問題未解，
投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.27
能源、AI價格飆升提振中國相關行業利潤增速

中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
• 中國科技公司享受負殖利率差：中國在經濟成長放緩與寬鬆貨幣
政策下，借貸成本持續下降，10年期公債殖利率由年初1.86%降
至1.63%，使企業融資條件更加寬鬆，相較之下，美國受經濟韌
性與高利率政策影響，10年期公債殖利率升至約5%，企業整體融
資成本明顯提高。此外2025年中國大型科技企業在境內債券市場
融資規模有限，僅為約74億美元，而且支付的殖利率，平均比同
風 險 層 級 的 債 券 高 出18個 基 點，但現在溢 價 已 經 變 為 折 價 ，
2026年迄今，中國科技企業已在境內發債約100億美元，新發科
技債殖利率平均折價6個基點，顯示機構投資者信心發生了顯著轉
變，大幅降低了行業推進AI建設的資金成本，會有上述現象是由
於中國經濟通縮，難以承受更高利率，因此官方須維持較寬鬆的

金融條件，科技企業剛剛開始通過債券市場融資，預料債券將受
到投資者關注。
• 展望後市：低成本債券融資對中國科技行業構成利好，與IPO不
同，債券市場為企業提供了一個可重複利用的融資渠道，在股市
劇烈波動之際，對投資人是另一種資產配置，可優先關注具資金
優勢與應用場景的大型科技龍頭，以及受惠於AI算力需求成長的
伺服器、網通設備和資料中心產業。
中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
中美國債走勢分化
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.29

AI產業鏈融資警訊：資金缺口正在擴散
• 資本支出與現金流的嚴重失衡：AI生態系中的巨額投資正考驗著企業，一旦企業營運現金流不足以支持基礎設施建設，就必
須高度依賴外部融資(發債或增資)，使其更容易受到資本市場波動與利率變化的衝擊。AI算力提供商資金缺口最為顯著，其
資本支出已接近營運現金流3倍(284%)，需要投入大額資金採購高端GPU並對外出租算力，數據中心資本支出佔現金流的比
例已從2022年11月(ChatGPT問世)1倍(104%)快速爬升至1.3倍(128%)，超大規模雲服務商資本支出占現金流比例已從2022
年底40%翻倍飆升至73%。在彭博研究統計的8家超大型雲業者中，出現自由現金流為負(資金缺口)公司數量已從2022年的
1家增加至4家(達50%)。
• 產業鏈錯綜複雜，風險具連鎖效應：從上游晶片製造商、硬體開發商、建築商、電力/公用事業，到中游的數據中心與雲端

服務商，再到下游的軟體與數據管理公司，其銷售與支付合約彼此高度緊密相連。當算力提供商或數據中心出現資金吃緊時，
風險會迅速向上游傳遞，導致項目延期、融資成本攀升，甚至延後整個生態系的收入實現時程。目前除了雲端與數據中心外，
硬體開發商與建築商中出現資金缺口的企業比例也在上升。
資金缺口正蔓延至人工智能產業鏈一半的層級
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
算力提供商和數據中心的資本支出超過現金流

市場定價轉變：借貸成本上升與股票估值修正
• 資本市場已經開始針對資金缺口進行風險定價：在資金缺口最大的AI算力提供商、數據中心以及超大規模雲服務商中，其發
行的公司債相較於美國國債的殖利率差(利差)顯著拓寬，高於一般投資級債券或高收益債券的表現，顯示債券市場對其違約
與融資風險要求更高的風險補償。由於大量現金流被基礎設施建設掏空，且AI應用端的獲利回收速度未達市場預期，投資人
不再願意給予極高溢價，整體AI產業鏈的預期本益比出現明顯修正。
• 小結與投資建議：當前AI產業正在經歷基礎設施過渡期，資本密集的算力出租、數據中心建設正遭受強烈的資金擠壓，若未
來1~2年內下游應用端的商業化營收無法順利銜接，依賴高度槓桿與外部融資的企業將面臨嚴峻的流動性挑戰。對AI算力出
租商及部分高負債的數據中心開發商保持謹慎態度，一旦信貸市場收緊或借貸成本升高，利潤與營運將受重創。晶片製造商

的經營現金流持續成長，財務結構改善，選擇擁有強大訂價權與現金流護城河的上游半導體巨頭，安全邊際最高，電力與公
用事業資本支出佔現金流比例相對穩定且受惠於剛性需求。超大規模雲服務商中表現差異大，優先選擇資產負債表健全、資
產回報率高且擁有傳統核心業務能持續提供穩定現金流的科技巨頭，遠離需大量靠發債維持資本支出的個股。
超大規模雲服務商和數據中心的利差擴大
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
隨著借貸成本波動加劇，AI股權估值走弱

顧 • 利率可能維持高檔更久，但收益價值正在浮現：近月以
來美國10年期公債殖利率一度升抵5.36%，30年期接
近5.7%，反映市場重新評價未來利率中樞、財政赤字
及資金需求等因素。雖然長天期債券價格仍可能受到利
率波動影響，但高殖利率已大幅提高新進場資金的收益
起點，投資焦點應由預測利率轉向評估收益價值。
• 債券標售與資金流顯示配置需求逐步回溫：10月美國
財政部10年期、20年期及30年期公債標售需求維持穩
健，投標倍數約介於2.5至2.8倍區間；同時，美國債券
基金已連續26週呈現資金淨流入，顯示市場未必認為
利率已見頂，但已開始接受5%以上殖利率的長期配置
價值。
• 收益優先取代押注利率轉折：在Higher for Longer環
境下，債券的核心功能重新回到穩定收益、分散風險與

降低組合波動。建議可以3至7年期美國公債及高評等
投資級公司債作為核心收益部位，長天期債券則採分批
布局方式，兼顧收益來源及未來利率循環變化帶來的潛
在機會。

7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移

（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」

(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が 6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比 9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

2026年度

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％）、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％）、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％）、うち中国向けが

Document 132: 261113_ms_alchip

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_ms_alchip.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

MS Rating
▪ We continue to believe that AI ASICs will

outgrow AI GPUs in the coming years, and
Alchip is one of the few ASIC design service
providers that can deal with increasingly
complex designs (at 2nm) with good
production/design track records for large
leading edge chips.
▪ AWS's announcement of its 3nm
Trainium3 chip matches the timing of
Alchip's 3nm project win. The growing
number of use cases for Trainium (e.g.,
Apple's adoption) bodes well for Alchip's
revenue growth in 2026.
▪ We believe Alchip has strong potential to

win Trainium4.
▪ Our price target implies 26x our 2027e
EPS, which we ﬁnd undemanding vs. our
>100% revenue CAGR for 2025-28e.

NT$6,880.00
30x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to
proliferate, amid substantial end demand.

Alchip's leading-edge tape-out numbers
increase signiﬁcantly, with some high-proﬁle
project wins at 3nm or even 2nm. Alchip is
able to secure enough foundry capacity
(front-end wafers and back-end packaging)
and substrates for all of its leading-edge
turnkey production. Alchip gradually gains
market share within the design service
market, amid continued proliferation of
custom chip demand.
NT$5,888.00
26x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to

proliferate amid strong end demand,
resulting in steady growth in tape-outs and
volume. There might be a slowdown in
Alchip's Chinese customers' design activity
for leading-edge chips as a result of US
export controls; we believe this would be
largely offset by ASIC demand strength
from non-China regions.
NT$2,465.00
11x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects lack concrete
end demand, resulting in delayed tape-outs
and mass production. Alchip's leading-edge

project pipeline deteriorates, or some
customers turn directly to TSMC. AI GPU's
dominance in AI computing compresses the
market share gain potential for overall ASIC,
with customers starting to slow their
custom silicon developments - and therefore
ASIC procurement plans.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Merida hosted its 3Q26 investor call on 17 November after market hours, 
following release of its 3Q26 results on 11 November (see our memo). Merida’s gross 
margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our estimate 
(16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Factoring in the equity investment loss of 
TWD150m+, its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the 
consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results recap. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales, the price discount 
for further inventory digestion and the depreciation of the Euro. Factoring in the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus estimates by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was 
TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).  
• 2026 outlook update. Merida revised down its 2026 full-year shipments to 960k units 
(Taiwan plant 280k, down from previous 340k; China plant 680k, down from previous 
700k), as it continues to manage its existing model shipments and expects new models 
in 2027 to bring stronger demand. The company now sees recovery in Europe and 
China, with inventory digestion coming to an end: 9M26 sales in Europe grew 14% YoY, 
driven by strong demand for road bikes and gravel bikes; 9M 26 sales in China grew by 
1% while shipments grew by 7% (vs. revenue guidance of declining 15-20% at the 
beginning of 2026), driven by low- and mid-end models. 
• SBC expected to be profitable in 4Q26. Due to SBC’s conservative tax assumption 
(over 100% effective tax rate) in 1Q-3Q26, if and when the tax rate realised at year-end 
is determined to be less than currently allocated for, we expect there will be a huge profit 
recognised in 4Q26 to offset the estimated after-tax loss recognised in 1Q-3Q26. Merida 
and SBC will review the tax assumption policy after full-year results are out, as the 
policy has caused a great deal of investor confusion. Including USD15m of tariff refund 
in 3Q26, SBC’s pre-tax profit grew by 42% YoY for 9M26. Merida expects there is 
another USD5m of tariff refund to collect in 4Q26.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.2x/10.6x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER 
trading range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the 
EU and the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory 
digestion.

TSMC  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
AI demand and key strategy: TSMC remains confident in the AI mega trend.
Technology roadmap and capex capacity planning are the most critical decisions for
company's long-term development.
US power constraint: Regarding the US power infrastructure shortage concerns,
TSMC has its own internal assessment process and disciplines, and combining with
customers' feedback, there is no change in the demand forecast.

Capacity and US expansion: TSMC is still aiming to more than double its 2nm
capacity by next year, given strong demand from AI and smartphone. The company
also continues to evaluate the next US fab site, and there was a discussion with
Tesla for TeraFab cooperation in parallel.
GPU and ASIC customers: TSMC will continue support major GPU customers, as it
does with other customers' needs. Both GPU and ASIC are TSMC's customers, and
TSMC have been balancing customers' needs.

Pricing and margin: TSMC's pricing reflects its value and cost inflation. Although
semiconductor and foundry remain a cyclical industry, TSMC hopes its margin will
be consistent and sustainable through the cycle.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf

Pages: 11

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

 삼성전자 (005930) 
 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

### Page 2

Appendix

Document 291: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-09).pdf

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment."1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP. A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

Document 368: Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

#gdp #australia #employment #housing #rba #productivity
Productivity: reasons for optimism
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
08:37 November 17 2026 Share
The supply-side outlook is improving as capital deepening strengthens, reallocation drags
fade and AI diffusion gathers pace.
The supply-side outlook is looking brighter. A pickup in capital deepening and a fading ‘reallocation’
drag are already evident in the Annual National Accounts, with AI diffusion offering further upside.

This gives us greater conviction that productivity growth can return towards 1% or more over time,

### Page 2

Growth in 2024-25 was a little firmer than first thought
The 2025–26 figures show somewhat firmer economic activity and business investment than previously
estimated, particularly in 2024–25. The economy grew by 2.3% in year-average terms, broadly in line
with the initial quarterly National Accounts estimate of 2.4%. However, growth in 2024–25 was revised
up by 0.3ppts to 1.6%, largely reflecting stronger private business investment. Business investment is

now estimated to have increased by 1.1% in 2024–25, compared with the initial estimate of a 0.2%
decline. The revisions do not materially alter the broader productivity story, but they do suggest that the
economy was growing a little more strongly for the same rate of inflation in 2025.
above the RBA’s assumption and closer to Treasury’s. The recovery illustrates why it is important to
focus on longer-term trends in productivity growth rather than on the latest year or two, which can be

volatile.
Capital deepening is returning. Investment is picking up and, outside mining, market-sector capital
per hour worked is now at a record high, rising a strong 1.5% in 2025–26.
The reallocation headwind is easing. The shift of labour towards non-market industries, which
generally have lower measured productivity levels, has reduced the level of aggregate productivity by
around 1ppt over the past 15 years. With the expansion of the non-market sector plateauing, this

drag looks set to fade.
Productivity growth is about more than capital. Multifactor productivity, which captures how efficiently
businesses combine labour and capital, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth
in 2025–26. Meanwhile, stronger investment in IT, utilities and professional services should provide a
further lift as new capital is put to work.

### Page 3

The investment upswing is broadening
The Annuals also provide a richer picture of the supply side, including investment by industry and the
evolution of the capital stock, details that are not available in the quarterly accounts. These data point
to a notable lift in investment in the market (ex-mining) part of the economy, where investment has
gone from around 10% of nominal GDP in to just over 11.5%. Outside of this, investment has been
softer, particularly in the mining sector.

### Page 4

Within the market sector, investment by the IT industry, increasingly focused on data centres,
has risen to its highest share of nominal GDP since the technology boom of the 1990s. Utilities
investment, including renewables and associated infrastructure, is stronger still, at its highest share
since 1983, while investment by the professional services industry has reached a record high.

### Page 5

Across the non-market sector (including health, education and public administration), investment has
also held at a higher share of the economy, driven by the health and public administration (including
defence) industries.

### Page 6

Investment is translating into capital deepening
The investment upswing is also beginning to translate into capital deepening as industries look to
rebuild capital stocks after a period of soft growth. Outside mining, market-sector capital per hour
worked is at a record high and well above its pre-pandemic level. That is an encouraging
development, particularly given that the sector accounts for 70% of total employment.
There are important divergences beneath the aggregate. Mining continues to experience capital

shallowing, as investment has been insufficient to cover wear and tear and capital requirements
associated with additional workers. By contrast, the capital stock in the non-market sector appears to
have caught up with the surge in employment we have seen since the pandemic, with capital per
worker increasing for the first time since the pandemic.

### Page 7

A major productivity headwind is starting to fade
Capital deepening will help support productivity growth across the economy. The Annuals show that
productivity in the market sector (excluding mining) continues to grow by around 1% in year-average
terms, while mining remains a drag on aggregate productivity (down 0.8% in 2025–26 and almost 30%
since 2017).

### Page 8

At the same time, a major recent headwind is easing as the rapid expansion of the non-market sector
approaches slows. This matters for aggregate productivity. Productivity growth reflects both gains
within industries, through capital deepening, technology and better processes, and the ‘reallocation’ of
resources between industries with different productivity levels.
Measured productivity in the non-market sector is considerably lower than across much of the market

economy, partly reflecting measurement issues (output in these sectors is generally not well measured,
particularly when there are quality changes). As employment shifted towards these industries, this
compositional effect weighed on aggregate productivity. Our estimates suggest it has reduced the
level of aggregate productivity by a significant 1ppt over the past 15 years and almost 0.3ppts
over the three years to 2025–26.

### Page 9

Productivity gains were about more than capital
It wasn’t only capital deepening that drove productivity growth. Multifactor productivity (MFP), which
captures how efficiently businesses combine labour and capital through changes in processes,
technology, know-how, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth this year. This
gives us more conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve from
its recent lows.

### Page 10

Where productivity is improving, and where it isn’t
At the industry level, some of those recording stronger investment are also beginning to deliver
stronger productivity outcomes, although the picture remains uneven.
IT stands out at the positive end of the spectrum, alongside improving outcomes in manufacturing,
administrative and support services, and transport.. These are also industries where the diffusion of
AI and other digital technologies could deliver further productivity gains. Utilities and

professional services should also benefit as stronger investment translates into greater capital intensity
and efficiency.
At the other end, mining remains a major drag, consistent with the capital shallowing evident in the
investment and capital-stock data. Construction is another notable laggard, with productivity continuing
to deteriorate despite strong demand from housing, population growth, renewable-energy infrastructure
and data-centre projects. Construction productivity fell around 2% in 2025–26 and almost 21% over the

13 years from 2012–13 to 2025–26.

### Page 11

A more constructive supply-side story
The Annuals provide a more constructive supply-side picture than the aggregate productivity figures
alone suggest. Market-sector productivity outside mining continues to advance, the reallocation drag
from the expanding non-market sector looks likely to fade, and capital deepening is strengthening
across important parts of the economy. The strong MFP outcome in the market sector gives us more
conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve going ahead.

The critical question is whether the strong investment underway in technology, professional services,
electricity and related infrastructure translates into stronger productivity growth as that new capital is
put to work. If it does, the improvement in capital intensity evident in the Annuals could mark the
beginning of a more durable lift in Australia’s productivity performance.

### Page 12

Document 394: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15

Document 439: 【國泰證期研究部】衛星產業-星間鏈路ISL為邁向太空AI重要關鍵技術-20261119

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】衛星產業-星間鏈路ISL為邁向太空AI重要關鍵技術-20261119.pdf

產業更新 – 衛星產業 
星間鏈路 ISL 為邁向太空 AI 重要關鍵技術 
What’s New –  星間鏈路逐步成熟，帶動衛星傳輸效率提升 
隨著低軌衛星產業由早期單純提供地面通訊覆蓋，逐步朝向全球高速寬頻、即時資料傳輸及太
空運算網路發展，星間鏈路（Inter-Satellite Link, ISL）已從衛星系統中的輔助通訊技術，逐
漸演變為支撐大型衛星星座運作的核心基礎設施。透過在衛星之間建立直接通訊鏈路，使資料
得以在軌道上進行多節點轉送，再由適當的衛星回傳至地面，進而降低對密集地面站的依賴，
並提升網路覆蓋範圍、路由彈性及系統備援能力。相較傳統射頻通訊，雷射通訊具備高頻寬、
窄波束及較低跨鏈路干擾等優勢，成為新一代衛星星座的重要發展方向。 
Trends/Outlook – SpaceX 持續提升 ISL 規模 
Starlink 已建立全球規模的太空雷射網狀通訊架構，使衛星接收的資料能夠透過多顆衛星進行
跨區域轉送，降低對地面閘道站的依賴，並有效擴大海洋、極地及偏遠地區的通訊覆蓋能力。
就產品規格而言，目前 Starlink既有衛星 V2 mini採用每顆配置 3組光學雷射終端的架構，單
鏈路最高傳輸速率達 200Gbps，而新一代 V3衛星則進一步增加至 6組雷射終端，單鏈路傳輸
速率提升至 400Gbps，顯示 SpaceX正同步透過增加終端數量及提高傳輸頻寬，擴充整體星座
的資料交換能力。此外，SpaceX 亦逐步將原本供內部使用的雷射通訊技術推向外部商業市場，
推出傳輸速率達 25Gbps的 Mini Laser終端，使第三方衛星得以接入 Starlink網路，提供在軌
資料中繼及高速資料回傳服務。 
Investment Summary 
1. 展望未來，ISL 將成為推動衛星產業由通訊服務邁向太空資訊基礎設施的重要關鍵技術。
預期 SpaceX Starmind中的 AI1運算衛星在獨立衛星 AI節點進行計算後，其結果將透過
ISL雷射互傳形成 AI 叢集，方便將上百上千顆的衛星結果進行聯通，並且及時回傳於地面。
故整體太空 AI資料中心的運行將大幅仰賴 ISL技術的成熟，看好相關衛星通訊元件廠商如
萊德光電(7717 TT)、昇達科(3491 TT)後市發展。 
2. 預期 SpaceX 26/27年新增 2,800/4,000 顆衛星，其中 27年除通訊衛星 V3外，新一代直
連服務衛星亦將起量。SpaceX甫順利完成第 15次星艦試射，將加速 V3衛星部屬，故持
續看好天上衛星 CCL與 PCB商台光電(2383 TT)、華通(2313 TT)後市發展。

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/11/19

2 
光學星間鏈路逐步成熟，帶動衛星傳輸效率提升 
隨著低軌衛星（LEO）產業由早期單純提供地面通訊覆蓋，逐步朝向全球高速寬頻、即時資料傳輸
及太空運算網路發展，星間鏈路（Inter-Satellite Link, ISL）已從衛星系統中的輔助通訊技術，逐
漸演變為支撐大型衛星星座運作的核心基礎設施。過去傳統衛星通訊主要仰賴衛星與地面閘道站之
間建立連線，衛星接收的資料需先回傳至地面，再透過地面骨幹網路進行交換與轉送，因此服務範
圍容易受到地面站部署位置、地理環境及基礎建設覆蓋程度限制。隨著 Starlink等大型低軌衛星星
座加速部署，衛星數量持續增加，全球通訊需求亦逐步延伸至海洋、極地、航空及其他地面網路難
以覆蓋的區域，使傳統高度依賴地面站的傳輸架構逐漸面臨限制，遞訊號路徑如同光透過平面鏡反
射一樣稱為彎管連結，衛星必須同時能被終端設備與地面站接收到，造成偏遠地區、海洋上若無地
面站建置則無法達成衛星通訊服務。 
圖一：衛星間傳輸路徑比較                    
資料來源：前瞻科技管理、國泰證期研究部整理 
尤其隨著光學星間鏈路（Optical ISL）逐漸成熟，透過在衛星之間建立直接通訊鏈路，使資料得以
在軌道上進行多節點轉送，再由適當的衛星回傳至地面，進而降低對密集地面站的依賴，並提升網
路覆蓋範圍、路由彈性及系統備援能力。相較傳統射頻通訊，雷射通訊具備高頻寬、窄波束及較低
跨鏈路干擾等優勢，成為新一代衛星星座的重要發展方向。 
表一：衛星雷射通訊與傳統 RF 通訊對照 
比較項目 衛星雷射通訊（Lasercom） 傳統微波（RF）通訊 
技術原理 利用近紅外光傳輸數位訊號 利用無線電波（如 Ka/Ku 頻段）傳輸 
傳輸頻寬／速率 極高 中低 
頻譜限制 無限制 受限，需嚴格申請頻道，資源擁擠 
安全性 高 低 
固定距離通訊成本 低 高 
設備／架設成本 初期極高（高精密對準系統） 成熟且低（技術標準化、地面設備普及） 
主要缺點 易受氣候影響，對準精度要求極高 頻譜稀缺、延遲較高、易受電子干擾 
資料來源：國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/11/19

3 
新世代衛星、火箭升級短期影響衛星部屬速度 
SpaceX 為目前全球最積極部署 ISL 的衛星營運商之一，透過旗下 Starlink 低軌衛星星座，已將光
學星間通訊技術由早期研發驗證推進至大規模商業應用。SpaceX 最早於 2020 年開始進行衛星間
雷射通訊測試，並成功透過兩顆衛星傳輸大量數據；2021年 1月首次將搭載雷射星間鏈路的 10顆
Starlink衛星部署至極地軌道，隨後於同年 9月正式導入配備 ISL的 Starlink V1.5衛星，逐步將雷
射通訊技術擴展至大規模星座部署。  
隨著衛星數量持續增加，Starlink 已建立全球規模的太空雷射網狀通訊架構，使衛星接收的資料能
夠透過多顆衛星進行跨區域轉送，降低對地面閘道站的依賴，並有效擴大海洋、極地及偏遠地區的
通訊覆蓋能力。就產品規格而言，目前 Starlink既有衛星 V2 mini採用每顆配置 3組光學雷射終端
的架構，單鏈路最高傳輸速率達 200Gbps，而新一代 V3衛星則進一步增加至 6組雷射終端，單鏈
路傳輸速率提升至 400Gbps，顯示 SpaceX 正同步透過增加終端數量及提高傳輸頻寬，擴充整體
星座的資料交換能力。此外，SpaceX 亦逐步將原本供內部使用的雷射通訊技術推向外部商業市場，
推出傳輸速率達 25Gbps的 Mini Laser終端，使第三方衛星得以接入 Starlink網路，提供在軌資料
中繼及高速資料回傳服務。 
圖三：Starlink 雷射通訊系統示意圖 
資料來源：SpaceX、國泰證期研究部整理 
展望未來，隨著衛星直連終端、太空資料中心及軌道運算等應用逐步發展，ISL 的重要性將不再侷
限於改善地面網路覆蓋，而是進一步支援跨衛星資料交換、分散式運算及天地整合網路架構，成為
推動衛星產業由通訊服務邁向太空資訊基礎設施的重要關鍵技術。預期 SpaceX Starmind中的 AI1
運算衛星在獨立衛星 AI節點進行計算後，其結果再透過 ISL雷射互傳形成 AI 叢集，方便將上百上
千顆的衛星結果進行聯通，並且及時回傳於地面，故未來太空 AI資料中心的運行將大幅仰賴 ISL技
術的成熟。
